# radiance kernel libraries

Generated by `rad-kbench`. Every kernel every loaded library supplies, run on the same bytes
and timed in the same pass. Correctness, completeness and speed are three readings of one
run, because they are not separable: a library that is fastest on the ops it has and absent
on the rest is slower end to end than one that is uniformly mediocre.

- fixture: `data/kernels.rkb`, 537 case(s) recorded 2026-09-21 from `q38-27b-fp8-df2.rad`
- libraries: `libr4d@0.5.0 libref@0.1.0`
- reference: `libref` -- every correctness number is relative to it, so it has none of its own
- 1067 case(s) checked, 1067 passed, 0 failed, 7 skipped, 0 capped; worst rel_l2 4.270e-03

## Libraries

`ops` is how many of the 89-op vocabulary this library has a row for, and `graph` how many of
the 32 ops THIS container's declared graph asks for. The two differ on purpose: a library can
be complete for one model and full of holes for the next.

`rows` is how many kernel rows the library declares and `run` how many of them a case in this
pass actually reached. The gap is the real coverage number: a library can be complete for the
vocabulary, pass everything it ran, and still have rows nothing has executed since they were
written -- see **Rows nothing ran** below for which.

| library | version | domain | rows | run | ops | graph | cases | pass | fail | worst rel_l2 | median us |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `libr4d` | 0.5.0 | device+host | 118 | 36 of 118 | 79 of 89 | 32 of 32 | 537 | 537 | 0 | 4.27e-03 | 12.96 |
| `libref` (oracle) | 0.1.0 | host | 70 | 31 of 70 | 70 of 89 | 32 of 32 | 530 | 530 | 0 | 0.00e+00 | - |

Rows declaring an optional hook, which is what separates a library that merely runs from one
the core can tune, fuse and check against a container:

| library | tunable rows | fusion claims | weight layouts |
|---|---|---|---|
| `libr4d` | 33 | 5 | 18 |
| `libref` | 0 | 0 | 0 |

## Correctness

979 distinct case(s) over 1067 check(s). `n` is how many times the case ran -- one per layer,
typically -- and `rel_l2` is the WORST of them, because one layer disagreeing is the finding.
`(synth w)` means this op's weights are not in the container and were drawn instead, so the
case proves the arithmetic and not the artifact.

## Speed

> **These times rank kernels; they do not size a roofline.** Every launch in a timed
> run reuses one set of operands, so a weight that fits in the last-level cache is
> read from cache after the first launch, and a bandwidth-bound kernel can read
> several times its true rate. Ranking two kernels at one shape is what these times are
> for; a roofline is not.
>
> `operands` is the total the case ALLOCATED, not what the kernel read: a gather
> touches M rows of a table, not the table. Dividing the two columns produces a
> bandwidth figure this tool cannot stand behind, which is why it does not print one.
>
> The `libref` column is ONE untimed-warmup call, not a median of reps: it is there to
> show the order of magnitude a reference implementation costs, which is the reason the
> device kernels exist. It is not a measurement of a library anyone tunes.
>
> `spread` is how far the fastest and slowest timed rep were apart, as a percent of
> the median. Each case is warmed with untimed launches first, because these cards sit in
> a low-power state whenever nothing is running and the ramp back up is longer than a
> short timed window -- but a warm-up is not a guarantee, and a wide spread is what marks
> a number the card was still ramping through. Read a row with a large one as an order of
> magnitude, not a measurement.

## Memory safety

Every device allocation in this run -- every operand and every kernel's own scratch -- was
made with a poisoned 256-byte margin on both sides, the kernel was handed the middle, and the
margins were read back and verified after the checked launch. It catches an out-of-bounds
WRITE, which is the one that corrupts a neighbouring tensor and surfaces as wrong output three
ops later in a kernel that is correct. An out-of-bounds READ it cannot see.

AMD's amdgcn AddressSanitizer (`/opt/rocm/amdgcn/bitcode/asanrtl.bc`) is not an option on every
card: its shadow is page-fault based and needs `xnack+`, which RDNA does not implement --
`rocminfo` reports `XNACK enabled: NO` there. The margins are what replaces it.

- 537 case(s) ran inside guarded allocations
- 0 wrote outside one

## Not covered

A skip is a real answer: the tool declined rather than invent an extent, a dtype or an
oracle it does not have. Each line below is a gap in COVERAGE, not a failing kernel.

**Collective ops are not checked here and cannot be.** `all_reduce`, `all_gather` and the
fused all-reduce forms establish one instance across ranks through a rendezvous over peer
memory: a single process has nobody to reduce with, and a kernel waiting for a peer that
never arrives hangs rather than failing. They appear in the completeness matrix because the
rows exist; they carry no correctness or speed number because producing one needs a second
rank, and that is a different harness.

| what | cases |
|---|---|
| logits_gemm  (reference was recorded through logits_gemm_fp8's weight layout, which ref_logits_gemm does not implement; only that row can reproduce it) | 7 |

## Static and dynamic analysis

Advisory, and generated separately from the measurements above:
`tools/kbench-analysis.sh` runs on a machine that need not have a GPU.

### clang-tidy

`bugprone-*`, `clang-analyzer-*` and `performance-*` over every host translation unit
(`libref/`, `core/`, `tools/`). HIP units are excluded: clang-tidy cannot replay the
offload flags and reports `__device__` as an error rather than analysing the body.
